\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=\min\left\{1,\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}_{s}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}}_{s})}\cdot\exp\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}}_{s})\right)\right\}\quad\mathrm{where}\quad\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=a({\bm{z}}^{\prime}-({\bm{z}}_{s}-\tau\cdot s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})),{\bm{z}}_{s}),

the transition kernel for the projected implementation of Alg,
2
will be

\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}_{s+1})=\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}_{s+1})\cdot\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}_{s+1})+\left(1-\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}_{s+1}).

Ideally projected implementation of Alg.
2
.

In this condition, we know the accurate
g({\bm{z}})-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})
and
\nabla g({\bm{z}})
.
In this condition, the ULA step will provide

\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}=\mathcal{N}\left({\bm{z}}_{s}-\tau\cdot\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s}),2\tau\right)

(28)

with a density function

\tilde{q}_{*}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}}_{s}|{\bm{z}}_{s})=\varphi_{2\tau}\left(\tilde{{\bm{z}}}_{s}-\left(\tau\cdot\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right)\right).

Considering the projection operation, i.e., Step
5
in Alg
1
, the transition distribution becomes

\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\mathcal{A}})=\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}}\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})+\int_{{\mathcal{A}}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}}\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\mathcal{A}}).

(29)

Hence, a
1/2
-lazy version of the transition distribution becomes

\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}).

(30)

Then, with the following Metropolis-Hastings filter,

\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=\min\left\{1,\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}_{s}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}}_{s})}\cdot\exp\left(-\left(g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right)\right)\right\},

(31)

the transition kernel for the accurate projected update will be

\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}_{s+1})=\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}_{s+1})\cdot\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}_{s+1})+\left(1-\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}_{s+1}).

(32)

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose we have

\eta=\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{2L+1}{2L},

then the target distribution of the Inner MALA, i.e.,
p^{\leftarrow}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{x}}_{0})
will be
L
-strongly log-concave and
3L
-smooth for any given
{\bm{x}}_{0}
.

Proof.

Consider the energy function
g({\bm{z}})
of
p^{\leftarrow}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{x}}_{0})
, we have

g({\bm{z}})=f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\bm{z}})+\frac{\left\|{\bm{x}}_{0}-{\bm{z}}\cdot e^{-\eta}\right\|^{2}}{2(1-e^{-2\eta})}

whose Hessian matrix satisfies

\displaystyle\left(\frac{e^{-2\eta}}{(1-e^{-2\eta})}+L\right)\cdot{\bm{I}}\succeq\nabla^{2}g({\bm{z}})=\nabla^{2}f_{(K-k-1)}({\bm{z}})+\frac{e^{-2\eta}}{(1-e^{-2\eta})}\cdot{\bm{I}}\succeq\left(\frac{e^{-2\eta}}{(1-e^{-2\eta})}-L\right)\cdot{\bm{I}}.

Under these conditions, if we have

\eta\leq\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{2L+1}{2L}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\frac{e^{-2\eta}}{1-e^{-2\eta}}\geq 2L,

which means

\frac{3e^{-2\eta}}{2(1-e^{-2\eta})}\succeq\nabla^{2}g({\bm{z}})\succeq\frac{e^{-2\eta}}{2(1-e^{-2\eta})}.

For the analysis convenience, we set

\eta=\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{2L+1}{2L},

that is to say
g({\bm{z}})
is
L
-strongly convex and
3L
-smooth.
∎

C.1
Control the error from the projected transition kernel

Here, we consider the marginal distribution of
\{{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\}
and
\{\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{s}\}
to be the random process when Alg.
2
is implemented by the standard and projected version, respectively.
The underlying distributions of these two processes are denoted as
{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\sim\mu_{s}
and
\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{s}\sim\tilde{\mu}_{s}
, and we would like to upper bound
\mathrm{TV}\left(\mu_{S},\tilde{\mu}_{S}\right)
for any given
{\bm{x}}_{0}
.

Rewrite the formulation of
{\mathbf{z}}_{S}
, we have

{\mathbf{z}}_{S}=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\cdot{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right)+{\mathbf{z}}_{S}\cdot{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\not=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right)

where
{\bm{1}}(\cdot)
is the indicator function.
In this condition, for any set
{\mathcal{A}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{1}}\left(\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)\cdot{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right)+{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)\cdot{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\not=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{1}}\left(\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)-{\bm{1}}\left(\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)\cdot{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\not=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right)+{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)\cdot{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\not=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right),

which means

-{\bm{1}}\left(\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)\cdot{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\not=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right)\leq{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)-{\bm{1}}(\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}})\leq{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\in{\mathcal{A}}\right)\cdot{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\not=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right).

Therefore, the total variation distance between
\mu_{S}
and
\hat{\mu}_{S}
can be upper bounded with

\mathrm{TV}\left(\mu_{S},\tilde{\mu}_{S}\right)\leq\sup_{{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\mu_{S}({\mathcal{A}})-\tilde{\mu}_{S}({\mathcal{A}})\right|\leq{\bm{1}}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{S}\not=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}\right).

Hence, to require
\mathrm{TV}\left(\mu_{S},\tilde{\mu}_{S}\right)\leq\epsilon/4
a sufficient condition is to consider
\mathrm{Pr}[{\mathbf{z}}_{S}\not=\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{S}]
.
The next step is to show that, in Alg.
2
, the projected version generates the same outputs as that of the standard version with probability at least
1-\epsilon/4
.
It suffices to show that with probability at least
1-\epsilon/4
, projected MALA will accept all
S
iterates.
In this condition, let
\{{\bm{z}}_{1},{\bm{z}}_{2},\ldots,{\bm{z}}_{S}\}
be the iterates generated by the standard MALA (without the projection step), our goal is to prove that with probability at least
1-\epsilon/4
all
{\mathbf{z}}_{s}
stay inside the region
\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)
and
\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}-{\bm{z}}_{s-1}\right\|\leq r
for all
s\leq S
.
That means we need to prove the following two facts

1.

With probability at least
1-\epsilon/8
, all iterates stay inside the region
\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)
.

2.

With probability at least
1-\epsilon/8
,
\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{s-1}\right\|\leq r
for all
s\leq S
.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\mu_{S}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{S}
be distributions of the outputs of standard and projected implementation of Alg.
2
.
For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we set

R\geq\max\left\{8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}}{L^{2}}+\frac{d}{L}},63\cdot\sqrt{\frac{d}{L}\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}}\right\},\quad r\geq(\sqrt{2}+1)\cdot\sqrt{\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}

where
{\bm{z}}_{*}
is denoted as the global optimum of the energy function, i.e.,
g
, defined in Eq.
21
.
Suppose
\mathrm{P}(\|{\mathbf{z}}_{0}\|\geq R/2)\leq\epsilon/4
and set

\tau\leq\min\left\{\frac{d}{(3LR+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}})^{2}},\frac{16d}{L^{2}R^{2}}\right\}=\frac{d}{(3LR+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}})^{2}},

then we have

\mathrm{TV}\left(\mu_{S},\tilde{\mu}_{S}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}.

Proof.

We borrow the proof techniques provided in Lemma 6.1 of
[
37
]
to control the TVD gap between the standard and the projected implementation of Alg.
2
.

Particles stay inside
{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{0}},R)
.

We first consider the expectation of
\left\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\right\|^{2}
when
{\mathbf{z}}_{s}
is given, and have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\right\|^{2}\Big|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right]=\int\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}{\mathcal{T}}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})

(33)

\displaystyle=\int\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\cdot\left[{\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\cdot a_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})+\left(1-\int a_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}}){\mathcal{T}}^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)\delta_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}+\int\left(\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}-\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}\right)\cdot a_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}){\mathcal{T}}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}^{\prime}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\cdot

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}+\int\left(\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}-\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}\right)\cdot a_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})+\frac{1}{2}\cdot Q_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int\left(\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}-\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}\right)\cdot\min\left\{q({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}}_{s}),q({\bm{z}}_{s}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\cdot\exp\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}}_{s})\right)\right\}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\cdot q({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}}_{s})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime},

where the second equation follows from Eq.
27
, the forth equation follows from Eq.
25
and the fifth equation follows from Eq.
26
and Eq.
24
.
Note that
q({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}}_{s})
is a Gaussian-type distribution whose mean and variance are
{\bm{z}}_{s}-\tau\cdot s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})
and
2\tau
respectively.
It means

\int\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}\cdot q({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}}_{s})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}=\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}-\tau\cdot s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|^{2}+2\tau d.

(34)

Suppose
{\bm{z}}_{*}
is the global optimum of the function
g
due to Lemma
C.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}-\tau\cdot s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|^{2}=\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}-2\tau\cdot{\bm{z}}_{s}^{\top}s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})+\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|^{2}

(35)

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}-2\tau\cdot{\bm{z}}_{s}^{\top}\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})+2\tau\cdot{\bm{z}}_{s}^{\top}\left(s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right)+\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})+\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}-2\tau\cdot\left(\frac{L\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}}{2}-\frac{\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}}{2L}\right)+\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}+\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+2\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|^{2}+2\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(1-L\tau+\tau^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}+\tau\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}/L+(1+2\tau^{2})\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{score}}+2\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-L\tau+(1+36L^{2})\cdot\tau^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}+\tau\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}/L+4\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}+(1+2\tau^{2})\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{score}},

where the first inequality follows from the combination of
L
-strong convexity of
g
and Lemma
E.3
, the second inequality follows from the
3L
-smoothness of
g

The strong convexity and the smoothness of
g
follow from Lemma
C.1
.

Combining Eq.
33
, Eq.
34
and Eq.
35
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\right\|^{2}\Big|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{L\tau}{2}+\frac{1+36L^{2}}{2}\cdot\tau^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\tau}{2L}+2\tau^{2}\right)\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}+\frac{(1+2\tau^{2})\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}+\tau d.

By requiring
\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}\leq\tau\leq L/(2+72L^{2})<1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\right\|^{2}\Big|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{L\tau}{4}\right)\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}+\frac{\tau}{L}\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}+(2+d)\tau.

Suppose a radio
R
satisfies

R\geq 8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}}{L^{2}}+\frac{d}{L}}.

(36)

Then, if
\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|\geq R/2\geq 4\sqrt{\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}/L^{2}+d/L}
, it has

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}\geq 16\cdot\left(\frac{\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}}{L^{2}}+\frac{d}{L}\right)\geq\frac{8\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}}{L^{2}}+\frac{8\cdot(2+d)}{L}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad\frac{L\tau\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}}{8}\geq\frac{\tau}{L}\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}+(2+d)\tau

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\right\|^{2}\Big|{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\right]\leq\left(1-\frac{L\tau}{8}\right)\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|^{2}.

To prove
\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|\leq R
for all
s\leq S
, we only need to consider
{\bm{z}}_{s}
satisfying
\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|\geq 4\sqrt{\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}/L^{2}+d/L}
, otherwise
\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|\leq R/2\leq R
naturally holds.
Then, by the concavity of the function
\log(\cdot)
, for any
\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|\geq R/2
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\log(\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\|^{2})|{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\right]\leq\log\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\|^{2}|{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\right]\leq\log(1-\frac{L\tau}{4})+\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|^{2})\leq\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|^{2})-\frac{L\tau}{4}.

(37)

Consider the random variable

\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{s}\coloneqq{\bm{z}}_{s}-\tau\cdot s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})+\sqrt{2\tau}\cdot\xi\quad\mathrm{where}\quad\xi\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},{\bm{I}})

obtained by the transition kernel Eq.
23
,
Note that
\|\xi\|
is the square root of a
\chi(d)
random variable, which is subgaussian and satisfies

\mathbb{P}\left[\|\xi\|\geq\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2}t\right]\leq e^{-t^{2}}

for any
t\geq 0
.
Under these conditions, requiring

\tau\leq(3LR+G+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}})^{-2}\cdot d\quad\mathrm{where}\quad G\coloneqq\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|,

(38)

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\right\|-\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|\geq 3\sqrt{\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau}t\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left\|\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{s}\right\|-\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|\geq 3\sqrt{\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau}t\right]

(39)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\tau\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|+\sqrt{2\tau}\left\|\xi\right\|\geq 3\sqrt{\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau}t\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\sqrt{2\tau}\|\xi\|\geq\sqrt{2\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau}t\right]\leq e^{-t^{2}}.

In Eq.
39
, the first inequality follows from the definition of transition kernel
{\mathcal{T}}_{{\bm{z}}_{s}}
shown in Eq.
27
and the second inequality follows from

\left\|\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{s}\right\|-\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|\leq\tau\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|+\sqrt{2\tau}\left\|\xi\right\|.

According to the fact

\displaystyle\tau\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\tau\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})\right\|+\tau\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}\leq\tau\cdot\left(\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}_{s})-\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}\right)

(40)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tau\cdot\left(3L\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\right\|+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}\right)\leq\sqrt{\tau d}

where the second inequality follows from the smoothness of
g
, and the last inequality follows from Eq.
38
and
\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|\leq R
,
we have

\displaystyle 3\sqrt{\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau}t-\tau\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}_{s})\|\geq\sqrt{2\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau}t,

which implies the last inequality of Eq.
39
for all
t\geq 0
.
Furthermore, suppose
\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|\geq R/2
, it follows that

\displaystyle\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s+1}\|^{2})-\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|^{2})=2\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s+1}\|/\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|)\leq\|{\bm{z}}_{s+1}\|/\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|-1\leq\frac{2\|{\bm{z}}_{s+1}\|-2\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|}{R}.

Therefore, we have
\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s+1}\|^{2})-\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|^{2})
is also a sub-Gaussian random variable and satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\log(\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\|^{2})-\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|^{2})\geq 6R^{-1}\sqrt{\tau d}+4R^{-1}t\sqrt{\tau}\right]\leq\exp(-t^{2}).

(41)

We consider any subsequence among
\{{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{S}
, with all iterates, except the first one, staying outside the region
\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R/2)
.
Denote such subsequence by
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\}_{s=0}^{S^{\prime}}
where
\|{\mathbf{y}}_{0}\|\leq R/2
and
S^{\prime}\leq S
.
Then, we know
{\mathbf{y}}_{s}
and
{\mathbf{y}}_{s+1}
satisfy Eq.
37
and Eq.
41
for all
s\geq 1
.
Under these conditions, by requiring
\|{\mathbf{z}}_{0}\|\leq R/2
with a probability at least
1-\epsilon/16
, we only need to prove all points in
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\}_{s=0}^{S^{\prime}}
will stay inside the region
\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)
with probability at least
1-\epsilon/16
.

Then, set
\mathcal{E}_{s}
to be the event that

\mathcal{E}_{s}=\left\{\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s^{\prime}}\|\leq R,\forall s^{\prime}\leq s\right\},

which satisfies
\mathcal{E}_{s-1}\subseteq\mathcal{E}_{s}
.
Besides, suppose the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{s}=\{{\mathbf{y}}_{0},{\mathbf{y}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\}
, the sequence

\displaystyle\left\{{\bm{1}}(\mathcal{E}_{s-1})\cdot\left(\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\|^{2}+Ls\tau/4)\right)\right\}_{s=1,2,\ldots,S}

is a super-martingale, and the martingale difference has a subgaussian tail, i.e., for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s+1}\|^{2})+\frac{L(s+1)\tau}{4}-\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\|^{2})-\frac{Ls\tau}{4}\geq 7R^{-1}\sqrt{\tau d}+4R^{-1}t\sqrt{\tau d}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s+1}\|^{2})+\frac{L(s+1)\tau}{4}-\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\|^{2})-\frac{Ls\tau}{4}\geq 6R^{-1}\sqrt{\tau d}+4R^{-1}t\sqrt{\tau}+\frac{L\tau}{4}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\log(\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}\|^{2})-\log(\|{\bm{z}}_{s}\|^{2})\geq 6R^{-1}\sqrt{\tau d}+4R^{-1}t\sqrt{\tau}\right]\leq\exp(-t^{2}),

where the first inequality is established when

\frac{L\tau}{4}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\tau d}}{R}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\tau\leq\frac{16d}{L^{2}R^{2}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad d\geq 1.

(42)

Under these conditions, suppose

u=\frac{6\sqrt{\tau d}}{R}+\frac{4t\sqrt{\tau d}}{R}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad t=\frac{uR}{4\sqrt{\tau d}}-\frac{3}{2},

it implies

t^{2}\geq\frac{R^{2}u^{2}}{64\tau d}-1

which follows from the fact
(a-b)^{2}\geq a^{2}/4-b^{2}/3
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then, for any
u\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s+1}\|^{2})+\frac{L(s+1)\tau}{4}-\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\|^{2})-\frac{Ls\tau}{4}\geq u\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{R^{2}u^{2}}{64\tau d}+1\right)\leq 3\exp\left(-\frac{R^{2}u^{2}}{64\tau d}\right),

which implies that the martingale difference is subgaussian. Then by Theorem 2 in
[
27
]
, for any
s
, we have

\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\|^{2})+\frac{Ls\tau}{4}\leq\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{0}\|^{2})+\frac{74}{R}\cdot\sqrt{s\tau d\log(1/\epsilon^{\prime})}

with the probability at least
1-\epsilon^{\prime}
conditioned on
\mathcal{E}_{s-1}
.
Taking the union bound over all
s=1,2,\ldots,S^{\prime}\ (S^{\prime}\leq S)
and set
\epsilon=16\epsilon^{\prime}S^{\prime}
, we have with probability at least
1-\epsilon/16
, for all
s=1,2,\ldots,S^{\prime}
, it holds

\displaystyle\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\|^{2})\leq

\displaystyle 2\log(R/2)+\frac{74}{R}\cdot\sqrt{s\tau d\log(16S/\epsilon)}-\frac{Ls\tau}{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\log(R/2)+\frac{74^{2}\cdot d\log(16S/\epsilon)}{R^{2}L}.

By requiring

R\geq 63\cdot\sqrt{\frac{d}{L}\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\frac{74^{2}\cdot d\log(16S/\epsilon)}{R^{2}L}\leq 2\log 2,

(43)

we have
\log(\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\|^{2})\leq\log(R^{2})
, which is equivalent to
\|{\mathbf{y}}_{s}\|\leq R
.
Combining with the fact that with probability at least
1-\epsilon/16
the initial point
{\mathbf{y}}_{0}
stays inside
\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R/2)
, we can conclude that with probability at least
1-\epsilon/8
all iterates stay inside the region
\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)
.

The difference between
{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}
and
{\mathbf{z}}_{s}
is smaller than
r
.

In this paragraph, we aim to prove
\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}-{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\|\leq r
for all
s\leq S
.
Similar to the previous techniques, we consider

\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{s}\coloneqq{\mathbf{z}}_{s}-\tau\cdot s_{\theta}({\mathbf{z}}_{s})+\sqrt{2\tau}\cdot\xi\quad\mathrm{where}\quad\xi\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},{\bm{I}}).

According to the transition kernel Eq.
27
, it has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}-{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\|\geq r\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\|\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{s}-{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\|\geq r\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\tau\|s_{\theta}({\mathbf{z}}_{s})\|+\sqrt{2\tau}\|\xi\|\geq r\right]

(44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\|\xi\|\geq\frac{r-\tau\|s_{\theta}({\mathbf{z}}_{s})\|}{\sqrt{2\tau}}\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\|\xi\|\geq\frac{r-\sqrt{\tau d}\|}{\sqrt{2\tau}}\right]

where the second inequality follows from the triangle inequality, and the last inequality follows from Eq.
40
when the choice of
\tau
satisfies Eq.
38
.
Under these conditions, by choosing

r\geq(\sqrt{2}+1)\cdot\sqrt{\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau\log(8S/\epsilon)}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\frac{r-\sqrt{\tau d}}{\sqrt{2\tau}}\geq\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(8S/\epsilon)},

Eq.
44
becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}-{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\|\geq r\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\|\xi\|\geq\frac{r-\sqrt{\tau d}\|}{\sqrt{2\tau}}\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\|\xi\|\geq\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{2}\cdot\sqrt{\log(8S/\epsilon)}\right]\leq\frac{\epsilon}{8S},

which means

\mathbb{P}\left[\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}-{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\|\leq r\right]\geq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{8S}.

Taking union bound over all iterates, we know all particles satisfy the local condition, i.e.,
\|{\mathbf{z}}_{s+1}-{\mathbf{z}}_{s}\|\leq r
with the probability at least
1-\epsilon/8
.
Hence, the proof is completed.
∎

C.2
Control the error from the approximation of score and energy

Lemma C.3
.

Under Assumption
[A1]
–
[A2]
, we set

\eta=\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{2L+1}{2L}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad G\coloneqq\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we set

R\geq\max\left\{8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}}{L^{2}}+\frac{d}{L}},63\cdot\sqrt{\frac{d}{L}\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}}\right\},\quad r=3\cdot\sqrt{\tau d\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}.

Suppose it has

\frac{\delta}{16}\coloneqq\frac{3\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}\cdot\sqrt{\tau d\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}+\frac{\tau\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{score}}}{4}+\frac{\tau(3LR+G)\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}\leq\frac{1}{32}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\leq\frac{1}{10},

we have

\left(1-\delta-5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\leq\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\leq\left(1+\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}}).

for any set
{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(0,R)
and point
{\bm{z}}\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)
.

Proof.

Note that the Markov process defined by
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}(\cdot)
and
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\cdot)
are
1/2
-lazy.
We prove the lemma by considering two cases:
{\bm{z}}\not\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{A}}
.

When
{\bm{z}}\not\in{\mathcal{A}}
,

we have

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{Q}_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}.

Similarly, we have

\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}.

In this condition, we consider

\displaystyle 2\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})-2\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})=

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}+\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}+\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime},

which means

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})}{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\cdot\left(\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})-\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{Term\ 1}}

(45)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\left(\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}}_{\mathrm{Term\ 2}}.

First, we try to control Term 1, which can be achieved by investigating
\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})/{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}
as follows.

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}=\exp\left(-\frac{\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}-({\bm{z}}-\tau\cdot s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}))\right\|^{2}}{4\tau}+\frac{\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}-({\bm{z}}-\tau\cdot\nabla g({\bm{z}}))\right\|^{2}}{4\tau}\right),

In this condition, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}=

\displaystyle\exp\left((4\tau)^{-1}\cdot\left(-\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}}\right\|^{2}-2\tau\cdot\left({\bm{z}}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})-\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})\right\|^{2}\right.\right.

(46)

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}}\right\|^{2}+2\tau\cdot\left({\bm{z}}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}}\right)^{\top}\nabla g({\bm{z}})+\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}})^{\top}\left(-s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})+\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right)+\frac{\tau}{4}\left(-\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|^{2}\right)\right).

It means

\displaystyle\left|\ln\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}})^{\top}\left(-s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})+\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right)+\frac{\tau}{4}\left(-\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|^{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}}\right\|\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})-\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|+\frac{\tau}{4}\cdot\left[\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})+\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})-\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}}\right\|\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})-\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|+\frac{\tau}{4}\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})-\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|^{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}})-\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{r\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}+\frac{\tau\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{scroe}}}{4}+\frac{\tau(3LR+G)\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}

where the last inequality follows from the fact
{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}({\bm{z}},r)\cap\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)/\{{\bm{z}}\}
,
{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{0}},R)
and

\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|=\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})-\nabla g({\bm{0}})+\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\leq 3L\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}\right\|+G\leq 3LR+G.

According to the definition of
R
and r shown in Lemma
C.2
, we choose

\displaystyle r\coloneqq 3\cdot\sqrt{\tau}\cdot\sqrt{d\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}\geq(\sqrt{2}+1)\cdot\sqrt{\tau d}+2\sqrt{\tau\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}

Under this condition, we require

\frac{\delta}{16}\coloneqq\frac{3\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}\cdot\sqrt{\tau d\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}+\frac{\tau\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{score}}}{4}+\frac{\tau(3LR+G)\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}\leq\frac{1}{32},

(47)

then we have

\ln\left(1-\frac{\delta}{8}\right)\leq\ln\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\leq\ln\left(1+\frac{\delta}{8}\right)\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad 1-\frac{\delta}{8}<\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\leq 1+\frac{\delta}{8},

and

-\frac{\delta}{8}\leq\min_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}-1\leq\mathrm{Term\ 1}\leq\max_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}-1\leq\frac{\delta}{8}.

(48)

with the definition of
\mathrm{Term\ 1}
shown in Eq.
45
.

Then, we try to control
\mathrm{Term\ 2}
of Eq.
45
and have

\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\left(\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}=\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\left(\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}.

(49)

According to Eq.
48
, it has

1-\frac{\delta}{8}\leq\min_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\leq\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\leq\max_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\leq 1+\frac{\delta}{8},

(50)

then we can upper and lower bounding
\mathrm{Term\ 2}
by investigating
\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})/\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})
as follows

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\min\left\{1,\exp\left(-(g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-g({\bm{z}}))\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\right\},

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\min\left\{1,\exp\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}})\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\right\}.

In this condition, for any
0<\delta\leq 1
, we first consider two cases.
When

\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=1\leq\exp\left(-(g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-g({\bm{z}}))\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=\exp\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}})\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\leq 1,

we have

\underbrace{\frac{\exp\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}})\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}}{\exp\left(-(g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-g({\bm{z}}))\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}}}_{\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}}\leq\frac{\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}=\exp\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}})\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\leq 1.

(51)

Besides, when

\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=\exp\left(-(g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-g({\bm{z}}))\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\leq 1\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})=1\leq\exp\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}})\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})},

we have

1\leq\frac{\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}=\frac{1}{\exp\left(-(g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-g({\bm{z}}))\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}}\leq\underbrace{\frac{\exp\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}})\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}}{\exp\left(-(g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-g({\bm{z}}))\right)\cdot\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}}}_{\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}}.

(52)

Then, we start to consider finding the range of
\ln(\mathrm{Term\ 2.1})
as follows

\displaystyle\left|\ln\left(\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}\right)\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\left(-r_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime},{\bm{z}})+(g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-g({\bm{z}}))\right)+\ln\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}+\ln\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}\right|

(53)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}+\left|\ln\frac{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})}\right|+\left|\ln\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}\right|\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}+\frac{\delta}{16}+\left|\ln\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}\right|,

where the last inequality follows from Eq.
47
.
Besides, similar to Eq.
46
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}=

\displaystyle\exp\left((4\tau)^{-1}\cdot\left(-\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}-2\tau\cdot({\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime})^{\top}s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|^{2}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.+\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|^{2}+2\tau\cdot({\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime})^{\top}\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})+\tau^{2}\cdot\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|^{2}\right)\right),

which means

\displaystyle\left|\ln\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}({\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime})^{\top}(-s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})+\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime}))+\frac{\tau}{4}\left(-\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|^{2}+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|^{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|+\frac{\tau}{4}\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})+\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|+\frac{\tau}{4}\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|^{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|\cdot\left\|s_{\theta}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{r\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}+\frac{\tau\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{score}}}{4}+\frac{\tau(3LR+G)\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2},

where the last inequality follows from the fact
{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}({\bm{z}},r)\cap\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)/\{{\bm{z}}\}
and

\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right\|=\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\nabla g({\bm{0}})+\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\leq 3L\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|+G\leq 3LR+G.

Combining this result with Eq.
47
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\ln\frac{\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{q}_{*}({\bm{z}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})}\right|\leq\frac{\delta}{16}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\frac{\delta}{16}=\frac{3\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}\cdot\sqrt{\tau d\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}+\frac{\tau\epsilon^{2}_{\mathrm{score}}}{4}+\frac{\tau(3LR+G)\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}}{2}.

Plugging this result into Eq.
53
, it has

\left|\ln\left(\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}\right)\right|\leq\frac{\delta}{8}+\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}.

By requiring
\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\leq 0.1
, we have

\displaystyle\ln\left(1-\frac{\delta}{4}-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\right)\leq\ln\left(\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}\right)\leq\ln\left(1+\frac{\delta}{4}+2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\right)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad 1-\frac{\delta}{4}-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\leq\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}\leq 1+\frac{\delta}{4}+2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}.

Combining this result with Eq.
51
and Eq.
52
, we have

1-\frac{\delta}{4}-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\leq\frac{a_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{a_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}\leq 1+\frac{\delta}{4}+2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}},

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\left(\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\geq\min_{z^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\frac{\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}-1\geq-\frac{\delta}{4}-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}

(54)

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\left(\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})-\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{{\mathcal{A}}\cap\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\tilde{q}({\bm{z}}^{\prime}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\leq\max_{z^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\frac{\tilde{a}_{{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}{\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}-1\leq\frac{\delta}{4}+2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}.

Plugging Eq.
54
and Eq.
50
into Eq.
49
, we have

-\frac{\delta}{3}-\frac{5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}\leq\left(-\frac{\delta}{4}-2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\right)\cdot\left(1+\frac{\delta}{8}\right)\leq\mathrm{Term\ 2}\leq\left(\frac{\delta}{4}+2\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\right)\cdot\left(1+\frac{\delta}{8}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{3}+\frac{5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}.

(55)

In this condition, combining Eq.
55
, Eq.
48
with Eq.
45
, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}\leq\frac{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})}{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})}\leq\frac{\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}

(56)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\quad\left(1-\frac{\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})\leq\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})\leq\left(1+\frac{\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}}).

Hence, we complete the proof for
{\bm{z}}\not\in{\mathcal{A}}
.

When
{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{A}}
,

suppose there exist some
r^{\prime}
satisfying

\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{B}({\bm{z}},r^{\prime})\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}.

We can split
{\mathcal{A}}
into
{\mathcal{A}}-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}}
and
\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}}
.
Note that by our results in the first case, we have

\left(1-\frac{\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}}-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\leq\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}}-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\leq\left(1+\frac{\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}}-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}}).

Then for the set
\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}\right|=\left|\frac{\left(1-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\right)-\left(1-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\right)}{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}\right|\leq\left|\frac{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}\right|\cdot\left|\frac{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}{\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}}{2}\cdot 2=\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}},

where the last inequality follows from Eq.
56
and the property of
1/2
lazy, i.e.,

\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega-\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\leq 1\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\geq\frac{1}{2}.

In this condition, we have

\left(1-\delta-5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\leq\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}})\leq\left(1+\delta+5\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\Omega^{\prime}_{{\bm{z}}}).

Hence, we complete the proof for
{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{A}}
.
∎

Corollary C.4
.

Under the same conditions as shown in Lemma
C.3
, if we require

\epsilon_{\mathrm{energy}}\leq\delta/5,

then we have

\left(1-2\delta\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})\leq\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})\leq\left(1+2\delta\right)\cdot\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}}),

for any set
{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\mathcal{B}(0,R)
and point
{\bm{z}}\in\mathcal{B}(0,R)
.

C.3
Control the error from Inner MALA to its stationary

In this section, we denote the ideally projected implementation of Alg.
2
whose Markov process, transition kernel, and particles’ underlying distributions are denoted as
\{\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{*,s}\}_{s=0}^{S}
, Eq.
32
, and
\tilde{\mu}_{*,s}
respectively.
According to
[
37
]
, we know the stationary distribution of the time-reversible process
\{\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{*,s}\}_{s=0}^{S}
is

\tilde{\mu}_{*}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}})=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\frac{e^{-g({\bm{z}})}}{\int_{\Omega}e^{-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}&&\quad{\bm{x}}\in\Omega;\\
&0&&\quad\text{otherwise}.\end{aligned}\right.

(57)

Here, we denote
\Omega=\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)
and

\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}=\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)\cap\mathcal{B}({\bm{z}},r).

In the following analysis, we default

\eta=\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{2L+1}{2L}.

Under this condition, the smoothness of
g
is
3L
and the strong convexity constant is
L
.

we aim to build the connection between the underlying distribution of the output particles obtained by projected Alg
2
, i.e.,
\tilde{\mu}_{S}
, and the stationary distribution
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
though the process
\{\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{*,s}\}_{s=0}^{S}
.
Since the ideally projected implementation of Alg.
2
is similar to standard MALA except for the projection, we prove its convergence through its conductance properties, which can be deduced by the Cheeger isoperimetric inequality of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.

Under these conditions, we organize this subsection in the following three steps:

1.

Find the Cheeger isoperimetric inequality of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.

2.

Find the conductance properties of
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*}
.

3.

Build the connection between
\tilde{\mu}_{S}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
through the process
\{\tilde{{\mathbf{z}}}_{*,s}\}_{s=0}^{S}
.

C.3.1
The Cheeger isoperimetric inequality of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}

Definition 1

(Definition 2.5.9 in
[
12
]
)
.

A probability measure
\mu
defined on a Polish space
({\mathcal{X}},\mathrm{dis})
satisfies a Cheeger isoperimetric inequality with constant
\rho>0
if for all Borel set

A\subseteq{\mathcal{X}}
, it has

\lim\inf_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\frac{\mu(A^{\epsilon})-\mu(A)}{\epsilon}\geq\frac{1}{\rho}\mu(A)\mu(A^{c}).

Lemma C.5

(Theorem 2.5.14 in
[
12
]
)
.

Let
\mu\in{\mathcal{P}}_{1}({\mathcal{X}})
and let
\mathrm{Ch}>0
. The following are equivalent.

1.

\mu
satisfies a Cheeger isoperimetric inequality with constant
\mathrm{Ch}
.

2.

For all Lipschitz
f\colon{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left|f-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}f\right|\leq 2\rho\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\left\|\nabla f\right\|

(58)

Remark 2
.

For a general non-log-concave distribution, a tight bound on the Cheeger constant can hardly be provided.
However, considering the Cheeger isoperimetric inequality is stronger than the Poincaré inequality,
[
6
]
lower bound the Cheeger constant
\rho
with
\Omega(d^{1/2}c_{P})
where
c_{P}
is the Poincaré constant of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.
The lower bound of
c_{P}
can be generally obtained by the Bakry-Emery criterion and achieve
\exp(-\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(d))
.
While for target distributions with better properties,
\rho
can usually be much better.
When the target distribution is a mixture of strongly log-concave distributions, the lower bound of
\rho
can achieve
1/\mathrm{poly}(d)
by
[
18
]
.
For log-concave distributions,
[
19
]
proved that
\rho=\Omega(1/(\Tr(\Sigma^{2}))^{1/4})
, where
\Sigma
is the covariance matrix of the distribution
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.
When the target distribution is
m
-strongly log-concave, based on
[
14
]
,
\rho
can even achieve
\Omega(\sqrt{L})
.
In the following, we will prove that the Cheeger constant can be independent of
x_{0}
.

Lemma C.6
.

Suppose
\mu_{*}
and
\tilde{\mu_{*}}
are defined as Eq.
21
and Eq.
57
, respectively, where
R
in
\tilde{\mu_{*}}
is chosen as that in Lemma
C.2
.
For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{\int_{\Omega}\tilde{\mu}_{*}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}})}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mu_{*}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}})}\leq 1.

Proof.

Suppose
\mu_{*}\propto\exp(-g)
and
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
are the original and truncated target distributions of the inner loops.
Following from Lemma
C.13
, it has

\mathrm{TV}\left(\mu_{*},\tilde{\mu}_{*}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}

when
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
is deduced by the
R
shown in Lemma
C.2
.
Under these conditions, supposing
\Omega=\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{*},\mu\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\mu_{*}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}})-\tilde{\mu}_{*}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}})\right|=\int_{\Omega}\left|\mu_{*}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}})-\tilde{\mu}_{*}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}})\right|+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega}\mu_{*}(\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}})

(59)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left|\frac{\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}})\right)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}-\frac{\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}})\right)}{\int_{\Omega}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right|\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega}\frac{\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}})\right)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}.

Suppose

Z=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad Z_{\Omega}=\int_{\Omega}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}},

then the first term of RHS of Eq.
59
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left|\frac{\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}})\right)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}-\frac{\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}})\right)}{\int_{\Omega}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right|\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\int_{\Omega}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\int_{\Omega}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}=1-\frac{Z_{\Omega}}{Z}

and the second term satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega}\frac{\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}})\right)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}-\int_{\Omega}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-g({\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}=1-\frac{Z_{\Omega}}{Z}.

Combining all these things, we have

2\cdot\left(1-\frac{Z_{\Omega}}{Z}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{Z_{\Omega}}{Z}\leq 1

where we suppose
\epsilon\leq 1
without loss of generality.
Hence, the proof is completed.
∎

Lemma C.7
.

Suppose
\mu_{*}
,
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
and
\epsilon
are under the same settings as those in Lemma
C.6
, the variance of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
can be upper bounded by
2d/L
.

Proof.

According to the fact that
\mu_{*}
is a
L
-strongly log-concave distribution defined on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with the mean
{\bm{v}}_{m}
, which satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mu({\bm{z}})\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{m}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}\leq\frac{d}{L}

following from Lemma
E.8
.
Suppose

\Omega=\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R),\quad Z=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}},\quad Z_{\Omega}=\int_{\Omega}\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}

where
R
shown in Lemma
C.2
, then the variance bound can be reformulated as

\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z}\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{m}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z}\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{m}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}\leq\frac{d}{L},

which implies

\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{m}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}\leq\frac{Z}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\frac{d}{L}\leq\frac{2d}{L}.

(60)

Note that the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.6
.
Besides, suppose the mean of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
is
{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}
, then we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{m}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}+{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}-{\bm{v}}_{m}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}

(61)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}+2\cdot\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left<{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}},{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}-{\bm{v}}_{m}\right>\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{v}}_{m}-{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}+\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{v}}_{m}-{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}

Combining Eq.
60
and Eq.
61
, the variance of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
satisfies

\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}\leq\frac{2d}{L}.

Hence, the proof is completed.
∎

Corollary C.8
.

For each truncated target distribution defined as Eq.
57
, their Cheeger constant can be lower bounded by
\rho=\Omega(\sqrt{L/d})
.

Proof.

It can be easily found that
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
is log-concave distribution, which means their Cheeger constant can be upper bounded by
\rho=\Omega(1/(\Tr(\Sigma))^{1/2})
, where
\Sigma
is the covariance matrix of the distribution
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.
Under these conditions, we have

\Tr\left(\Sigma\right)=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\exp(-g({\bm{z}}))}{Z_{\Omega}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{v}}_{\tilde{m}}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\bm{z}}\leq\frac{2d}{L},

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.7
.
Hence,
\rho=\Omega(\sqrt{L/d})
and the proof is completed.
∎

C.3.2
The conductance properties of
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*}

We prove the conductance properties of
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}
with the following lemma.

Lemma C.9

(Lemma 13 in
[
20
]
)
.

Let
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}
be a be a time-reversible Markov chain on
\Omega
with stationary distribution
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.
Fix any
\Delta>0
, suppose for any
{\bm{z}},{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in\Omega
with
\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\|\leq\Delta
we have
\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}},\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right)\leq 0.99
, then the conductance of
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}
satisfies
\phi\geq C\rho\Delta
for some absolute constant
C
, where
\rho
is the Cheeger constant of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.

In order to apply Lemma
C.9
, we have known the Cheeger constant of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
is
\rho
.
We only need to verify the corresponding condition, i.e., proving that as long as
\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\|\leq\Delta
, we have
\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}},\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right)\leq 0.99
for some
\Delta
.
Recalling Eq.
32
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})=

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})\cdot\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\left(1-\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})

(62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}\delta_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right)\cdot\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\left[1-\int\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}\delta_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)\right]\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}\delta_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right)\cdot\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\bm{z}})-\frac{1}{2}\int\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\cdot\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\int\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\cdot\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)\cdot\delta_{{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\tilde{Q}^{\prime}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})\cdot\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})\cdot\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\cdot{\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}\right],

where the second inequality follows from Eq.
30
and the last inequality follows from Eq.
29
.
Then the rest will be proving the upper bound of
\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}},\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right)
, and we state another two useful lemmas as follows.

Lemma C.10

(Lemma B.6 in
[
37
]
)
.

For any two points
{\bm{z}},{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, it holds that

\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\cdot),\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\cdot)\right)\leq\frac{(1+3L\tau)\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|}{\sqrt{2\tau}}

Proof.

This lemma can be easily obtained by plugging the smoothness of
g
, i.e.,
3L
, into Lemma B.6 in
[
37
]
.
∎

Corollary C.11

(Variant of Lemma 6.5 in
[
37
]
)
.

Under Assumption
[A1]
–
[A2]
, we set

\eta=\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{2L+1}{2L}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad G\coloneqq\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|.

If we set

\tau\leq\frac{1}{16\cdot(3LR+G+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}})^{2}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad r=3\cdot\sqrt{\tau d\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}

there exist absolute constants
c_{0}
, such that

\phi\geq c_{0}\rho\sqrt{\tau}
where
\rho
is the Cheeger constant of the distribution
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.

Proof.

By the definition of total variation distance, there exists a set
{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\Omega
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\cdot),\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\cdot)\right)=

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}})\right|.

Due to the closed form of
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}
shown in Eq.
62
, we have

\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})=\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\cdot{\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}\right]\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})

Under this condition, we have

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}({\mathcal{A}})-\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{A}})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\max_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\tilde{Q}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)}_{\mathrm{Term\ 1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\left|\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\cdot{\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}\right]\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})-\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\cdot{\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right]\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right|}_{\mathrm{Term\ 2}}.

Upper bound
\mathrm{Term\ 1}
.

We first consider to lower bound
\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})
in the following.
According to Eq.
31
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\geq\exp\left(-g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})-\frac{\left\|\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\tilde{{\bm{z}}}+\tau\nabla g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}}{4\tau}+g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\frac{\left\|\tilde{{\bm{z}}}-\hat{{\bm{z}}}+\tau\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}}{4\tau}\right),

which means

\displaystyle 4\ln\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\geq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\tau\cdot\left(\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}-\left\|\nabla g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}\right)}_{\mathrm{Term\ 1.1}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-2\cdot\left(g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})-g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})-\left<\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}}),\tilde{{\bm{z}}}-\hat{{\bm{z}}}\right>\right)}_{\mathrm{Term\ 1.2}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{+2\cdot\left(g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})-g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})-\left<\nabla g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}}),\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\right>\right)}_{\mathrm{Term\ 1.3}}.

Since
\mathrm{Term\ 1.2}
and
\mathrm{Term\ 1.3}
are grouped to more easily apply the strong convexity and smoothness of
g
(Lemma
C.1
),
it has

\mathrm{Term\ 1.2}\geq-3L\left\|\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\right\|^{2}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\mathrm{Term\ 1.3}\geq L\left\|\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\right\|^{2}\geq 0.

Besides, by requiring
\tau\leq 1/3L
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Term\ 1.1}=

\displaystyle\tau\cdot\left<\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})-\nabla g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}}),\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\nabla g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right>

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\tau\cdot\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})-\nabla g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right\|\cdot\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\nabla g(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-3L\tau\left\|\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\right\|\cdot\left(2\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|+3L\left\|\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\right\|\right)\geq-3L\tau^{2}\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}-6L\left\|\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\right\|^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle 4\ln\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\geq

\displaystyle-3L\tau^{2}\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}-9L\left\|\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\right\|^{2}=-3L\tau^{2}\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}-9L\left\|\tau\cdot\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})+\sqrt{2\tau}\cdot\xi\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-21L\tau^{2}\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}-36L\tau\left\|\xi\right\|^{2},

and

\ln\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\geq-6L\tau^{2}\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|^{2}-9L\tau\left\|\xi\right\|^{2}\geq-6L\tau^{2}\cdot\left(3LR+\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|\right)^{2}-9L\tau\|\xi\|^{2}

where the last inequality follows from

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right\|\leq\left\|\nabla g(\hat{{\bm{z}}})-\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\leq 3LR+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|.

Under these conditions, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Term\ 1}\leq

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{2}\cdot\exp\left(-6L\tau^{2}\left(3LR+\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|\right)^{2}\right)\cdot\min\int_{\Omega_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}}\exp\left(-9L\tau\|\xi\|^{2}\right)\cdot\tilde{q}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}}

(63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{2}\cdot\exp\left(-6L\tau^{2}\left(3LR+\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|\right)^{2}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},{\bm{I}})}\left[\exp\left(-9L\tau\|\xi\|^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1-0.4\cdot\exp\left(-6L\tau^{2}\left(3LR+\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|\right)^{2}\right)\cdot\exp\left(-18L\tau d\right),

where the last inequality follows from the Markov inequality shown in the following

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},{\bm{I}})}\left[\exp\left(-9L\tau\|\xi\|^{2}\right)\right]\geq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-18L\tau d\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},{\bm{I}})}\left[\exp\left(-9L\tau\|\xi\|^{2}\right)\geq\exp\left(-18L\tau d\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-18L\tau d\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}_{\xi\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},{\bm{I}})}\left[\|\xi\|^{2}\leq 2d\right]\geq\exp\left(-18L\tau d\right)\cdot\left(1-\exp(-d/2)\right).

Then, by choosing

\tau\leq\frac{1}{16\sqrt{L}\cdot\left(3LR+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\right)},

(64)

it has
6L\tau^{2}\left(3LR+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\right)^{2}\leq 1/40
.
Besides by choosing

\tau\leq\frac{1}{L^{2}R^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{40\cdot 18L\cdot\left(\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{\ln\frac{16S}{\epsilon}}\right)^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{40\cdot 18L\cdot d},

(65)

where the last inequality follows from the range of
R
shown in Lemma
C.2
,
it has
18Ld\tau\leq 1/40
.
Under these conditions, considering Eq.
63
, we have

\mathrm{Term\ 1}\leq 1-0.5\cdot\min_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega,\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}}\int_{\Omega_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\tilde{Q}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\leq 1-0.4\cdot e^{-1/20}.

(66)

Then, combining the step size choices of Eq.
64
, Eq.
65
, and Lemma
C.2
,
since the requirement

\tau\leq\frac{1}{16\sqrt{L}\cdot\left(3LR+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\right)},\quad\tau\leq\frac{1}{L^{2}R^{2}}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\tau\leq\frac{d}{(3LR+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}})^{2}}

can be achieved by

\tau\leq{16^{-1}\cdot(3LR+\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}})^{-2}},

(67)

the range of
\tau
can be determined.

Upper bound
\mathrm{Term\ 2}
.

In This part, we use similar techniques as those shown in Lemma 6.5 of
[
37
]
.
According to the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle 2\cdot\mathrm{Term\ 2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\left(1-\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right)\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}){\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}\right]\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}+\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\left(1-\tilde{a}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\hat{{\bm{z}}})\right)\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}){\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right]\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}

(68)

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\left(\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}){\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}\right]-\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}){\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right]\right)\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\cdot\left(1-\min_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega,\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}}\int_{\Omega_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\tilde{Q}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\left(\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}){\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}\right]-\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime}){\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right]\right)\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}\right|}_{\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}}.

Then, we upper bound
\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}}{\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right]\cdot\left(\tilde{q}_{(}\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}})-\tilde{q}_{(}\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\right)\right|+\left|\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in{\mathcal{A}}}\left({\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}\right]-{\bm{1}}\left[\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}\right]\right)\cdot\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\cdot),\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\cdot)\right)+\max\left\{\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}},\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}}\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\cdot),\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\cdot)\right)+\max\left\{\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}},\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}}\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}\right\}

According to the definition,
\tilde{q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\cdot)
is Gaussian distribution with mean
{\bm{z}}-\tau\nabla g({\bm{z}})
and covariance matrix
2\tau{\bm{I}}
, thus we have

\displaystyle\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}\leq\mathbb{P}_{\hat{{\mathbf{z}}}\sim\chi_{d}^{2}}\left[\hat{{\mathbf{z}}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(r-\tau\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|\right)^{2}/\tau\right]

\displaystyle\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}}\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}\leq\mathbb{P}_{\hat{{\mathbf{z}}}\sim\chi_{d}^{2}}\left[\hat{{\mathbf{z}}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(r-\tau\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|-\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|\right)^{2}/\tau\right].

Then, we start to lower bound

r-\tau\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|-\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|.

Then, we require

\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|\leq 0.1r\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\tau\leq\frac{d}{35\cdot\left(3LR+G\right)^{2}}

(69)

where the latter condition can be easily covered by the choice in Eq.
67
when
d\geq 3
without loss of generality.
Under this condition, we have

\displaystyle\tau\leq(0.17)^{2}\cdot\frac{d}{(3LR+G)^{2}}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\sqrt{\tau}\leq\frac{0.17\sqrt{d}}{3LR+G}.

(70)

Since we have

\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|=\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})-\nabla g({\bm{0}})+\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\leq 3L\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}\right\|+G\leq 3LR+G,

by the smoothness, it has

\sqrt{\tau}\leq\frac{0.17\sqrt{d}}{\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\tau\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|\leq 0.17\sqrt{\tau d}

(71)

Plugging Eq.
71
and Eq.
70
into Eq.
69
, we have

r-\tau\left\|\nabla g({\bm{z}})\right\|-\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|\geq 0.9r-0.17\sqrt{\tau d}\geq\sqrt{6.4\tau d}

where the last inequality follows from the choice of
r
shown in Lemma
C.3
, i.e.,

r=3\cdot\sqrt{\tau d\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}\geq 3\cdot\sqrt{\tau d}.

Under these conditions, we have

\max\left\{\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}}}\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}})\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}},\int_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}-\Omega_{{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}}\tilde{q}(\hat{{\bm{z}}}|{\bm{z}}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\hat{{\bm{z}}}\right\}\leq\mathbb{P}_{\hat{{\mathbf{z}}}\sim\chi_{d}^{2}}\left(\|{\mathbf{z}}\|\geq 3.2d\right)\leq 0.1.

Then combine the above results and apply Lemma
C.10
, assume
\tau\leq 1/(3L)
, we have

\mathrm{Term\ 2.1}\leq 0.1+\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\cdot),\tilde{Q}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\cdot)\right)\leq 0.1+\sqrt{2/\tau}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|

Plugging the above into Eq.
68
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Term\ 2}\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\min_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega,\tilde{{\bm{z}}}\in\Omega_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}}\int_{\Omega_{\hat{{\bm{z}}}}}\tilde{a}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\tilde{Q}_{*,\hat{{\bm{z}}}}(\mathrm{d}\tilde{{\bm{z}}})\right)+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\left(0.1+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\tau}}\cdot\left\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-0.8\cdot e^{-1/20}\right)+0.05+(2\tau)^{-1/2}\cdot\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\|,

where the second inequality follows from Eq.
66
.

After upper bounding
\mathrm{Term\ 1}
and
\mathrm{Term\ 2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}(\cdot),\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}^{\prime}}(\cdot)\right)\leq

\displaystyle 1-0.4\cdot e^{-1/20}+\left(1-0.8\cdot e^{-1/20}\right)+0.05+(2\tau)^{-1/2}\cdot\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0.91+(2\tau)^{-1/2}\cdot\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\|\leq 0.99

where the last inequality can be established by requiring
\|{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\|\leq\sqrt{2\tau}
.
Combining Lemma
C.9
, the conductance of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
satisfies

\phi\geq c_{0}\cdot\rho\sqrt{2\tau}.

Hence, the proof is completed.
∎

The connection between
\tilde{\mu}_{S}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.

With the conductance of truncated target distribution, we are able to find the convergence of the projected implementation of Alg.
2
.
Besides, the gap between the truncated target
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
and the true target
\mu_{*}
can be upper bounded by controlling
R
while such an
R
will be dominated by the range of
R
shown in Lemma
C.2
.
In this section, we will omit several details since many of them have been proven in
[
37
]
.

Lemma C.12

(Lemma 6.4 in
[
37
]
)
.

Let
\tilde{\mu}_{S}
be distributions of the outputs of the projected implementation of Alg.
2
.
Under Assumption
[A1]
–
[A2]
, if the transition kernel
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\bm{z}}}(\cdot)
is
\delta
-close to
\tilde{{\mathcal{T}}}_{*,{\bm{z}}}
with
\delta\leq\min\left\{1-\sqrt{2}/2,\phi/16\right\}
(
\phi
denotes the conductance of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
), then for any
\lambda
-warm start initial distribution with respect to
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
, it holds that

\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{S},\tilde{\mu}_{*}\right)\leq\lambda\cdot\left(1-\phi^{2}/8\right)^{S}+16\delta/\phi.

Lemma C.13

(Lemma 6.6 in
[
37
]
)
.

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, set
R
to make it satisfy

\mu\left({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{0}},R)\right)\geq 1-\frac{\epsilon}{12},

and
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
be the truncated target distribution of
\mu_{*}
.
Then the total variation distance between
\mu_{*}
and
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
can be upper bounded by
\mathrm{TV}\left(\tilde{\mu}_{*},\mu_{*}\right)\leq\epsilon/4
.

C.4
Main Theorems of InnerMALA implementation

Lemma C.14
.

Under Assumption
[A1]
–
[A2]
, we can upper bound
G=\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|
as

\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\leq L\cdot\sqrt{2(d+m_{2}^{2})}+3L\cdot\left\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\right\|.

Furthermore, we can reformulate
R
as

R=63\cdot\sqrt{(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2})\cdot\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}}

to make it satisfy the requirement shown in Lemma
C.3
.
Then, the range of inner step sizes, i.e.,
\tau
, will satisfy

\tau\leq C_{\tau}\cdot\left(L^{2}\left(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2}\right)\cdot\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}\right)^{-1},

where the absolute constant
C_{\tau}=2^{-4}\cdot 3^{-8}\cdot 7^{-2}
.

Proof.

To make the bound more explicit, we control
R
and
G
in our previous analysis.
For
G=\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|
, according to Eq.
21
, we have

\nabla g({\bm{z}})=\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\bm{z}})+\frac{e^{-2\eta}{\bm{z}}-e^{-\eta}{\bm{x}}_{0}}{(1-e^{-2\eta})},

which means

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\bm{0}})\right\|+\left\|\frac{e^{-\eta}{\bm{x}}_{0}}{1-e^{-2\eta}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\bm{0}})\right\|+\sqrt{\frac{2L}{2L+1}}\cdot(2L+1)\cdot\left\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\right\|\leq\left\|\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\bm{0}})\right\|+(2L+1)\cdot\left\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\right\|.

Besides, we should note
f_{(K-k-1)\eta}
is the smooth (Assumption
[A1]
) energy function of
p_{(K-k-1)\eta}
denoting the underlying distribution of time
(K-k-1)\eta
in the forward OU process.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{p_{(K-k-1)\eta}}\left[\left\|\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}\right]

(72)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{p_{(K-k-1)\eta}}\left[\left\|\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\mathbf{x}})\right\|^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}_{p_{(K-k-1)\eta}}\left[\left\|\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\mathbf{x}})-\nabla f_{(K-k-1)\eta}({\bm{0}})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2Ld+2L^{2}\mathbb{E}_{p_{(K-k-1)\eta}}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq 2Ld+2L^{2}\max\left\{d,m_{2}^{2}\right\}\leq 2L^{2}(d+m_{2}^{2})

where the first inequality follows from Lemma
E.6
, and the third inequality follows from Lemma
E.7
.
Under these conditions, we have

\left\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\right\|\leq L\cdot\sqrt{2(d+m_{2}^{2})}+3L\cdot\left\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\right\|.

(73)

Then, for
R
defined as

R\geq\max\left\{8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}}{L^{2}}+\frac{d}{L}},63\cdot\sqrt{\frac{d}{L}\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}}\right\},

we can choose
R
to be the upper bound of RHS.
Considering

8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\|\nabla g({\bm{0}})\|^{2}}{L^{2}}+\frac{d}{L}}\leq 8\cdot\sqrt{\frac{4L^{2}(d+m_{2}^{2})+18L^{2}\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2}}{L^{2}}+d}\leq 63\cdot\sqrt{(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2})},

then we choose

R=63\cdot\sqrt{(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2})\cdot\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}}.

After determining
R
, the choice of
\tau
can be relaxed to

\tau\leq C_{\tau}\cdot\left(L^{2}\left(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2}\right)\cdot\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}\right)^{-1},

where the absolute constant
C_{\tau}=2^{-4}\cdot 3^{-8}\cdot 7^{-2}
, since we have

\displaystyle\left(3LR+G+\epsilon_{\mathrm{score}}\right)^{2}\leq 9L^{2}R^{2}+4G^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 9L^{2}\cdot 63^{2}\cdot\left(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2}\right)\cdot\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}+4\left(4L^{2}\cdot\left(d+m_{2}^{2}\right)+18L^{2}\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 9\cdot 63^{2}\cdot L^{2}\left(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2}\right)\cdot\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}.

Hence, the proof is completed.
∎

Theorem C.15
.

Under Assumption
[A1]
–
[A2]
, for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, let
\tilde{\mu}_{*}({\bm{z}})\propto\exp(-g({\bm{z}})){\bm{1}}[{\bm{z}}\in\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)]
be the truncated target distribution in
\mathcal{B}({\bm{0}},R)
with

R=63\cdot\sqrt{(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2})\cdot\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}}=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left((d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2})^{1/2}\right),

r
in Alg.
2
satisfies

r=3\cdot\sqrt{\tau d\log\frac{8S}{\epsilon}}=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(\tau^{1/2}d^{1/2})

and
\rho
be the Cheeger constant of
\tilde{\mu}_{*}
.
Suppose
\tilde{\mu}_{0}(\{\|{\mathbf{x}}\|\geq R/2\})\leq\epsilon/16
, the step size satisfy

\tau\leq C_{\tau}\cdot\left(L^{2}\left(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2}\right)\cdot\log\frac{16S}{\epsilon}\right)^{-1}=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(L^{-2}\cdot(d+m_{2}^{2}+\|{\bm{x}}_{0}\|^{2})^{-1}),

the score and energy estimation errors satisfy
